Assessment and review A review of this information sharing agreement will take place after 6 months of implementation and then annually thereafter , unless otherwise agreed by the organisations Data Protection Officers . The aim of the review will be to ensure the purposes are still relevant , the scope has not slipped , the benefits to the data subjects and organisations are being realised , and the procedures followed for information security are effective . Changes in legislation and developments in the areas of public sector data sharing will be considered as and when they arise , as will any changes to the sign parties . The working group who drafted this agreement strongly recommend that a working group approach is used for any reviews , as this was a successful way to achieve pan - London and cross - special consensus to one sharing agreement .-->true_label--> <start> O O O O O O O B-ASSET O O B-RULE_TYPE O O B-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT O O O O B-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT O B-ASSET O O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O B-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE O O O O O O O O O O O B-ASSET O O O O O O O O O O O O B-ASSET O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O B-ASSET O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O B-PARTY_ASSIGNEE O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O-->predict_label--> <start> O O O O O O O B-ASSET O O B-RULE_TYPE O O B-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT O O O O B-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT O B-ASSET O O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O B-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE O O O O O O O O O O O B-ASSET O O O O O O O O O O O O B-ASSET O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O B-ASSET O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O B-PARTY_ASSIGNEE O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O

S any particular obligations on any individual Party : Incident Management The General Data Protection Regulation GDP as implemented by the UK Data Protection Act 2018 came into UK Law on 25 May 2018 . It introduces a duty on all organisations to report certain types of personal data breach to the relevant supervisor authority . The Security of Network and Information Systems Direct N Direct also requires reporting of relevant incidents to the Department of Health and Social Care as the competent authority from 10 May 2018 . An organisation must not a breach of personal data within 72 hours . If the breach is likely to result in a high risk to the rights and freedom of individuals , organisations must also inform those individuals without und delay . Those breach that also f the criteria of a N not incident will be forward to the Department of Health and Social Care where the Secretary of State is the competent authority for the implementation of the N direct in the health and social care sector . The Information Commissioner remains the national regulatory authority for the N direct . It is a legal obligation to not personal data breach of the General Data Protection Regulation under Article 33 within 72 hours , to the I , unless it is unlikely to result in a risk to the rights and freedom of individuals . Article 34 also makes it a legal obligation to communicate the breach to those affected . The Information Commissioner has asked all relevant health and social care organisations to use this reporting tool accessed via the Data Security and Protection Too in preference to the I provided Su of terrorist financing or money la Para 15 Use of D Schedule 1 Para 15 - This condition is met if the processing is necessary for the purposes of making a disclosure in good faith under a section 21 of the Terrorism Act 2000 disclosure between certain entities within regulated sector in relation to suspicion of commission of terrorist financing offence or for purposes of identifying terrorist property . For the purposes of law enforcement by competent authorities The competent authorities are defined in Section 30 of the D which refers to Schedule 7 . The competent authorities under this agreement are generally but not exclusively police , probation services , youth off teams and government departments . The law enforcement purposes are defined in Section 31 of the D as prevention , investigation , detection or prosecution of criminal offences or the execution of criminal penalties , including the safe against and the prevention of threats to public security . There are additional safe required for sensitive processing . This is defined in Section 35 as : a the processing of personal data revealing racial or ethnic origin , political opinions , religious or philosophical beliefs or trade union membership ; b the processing of genetic data , or of bio data , for the purpose of uniquely identifying an individual ; c the processing of data concerning health ; d the processing of data concerning an individuals sex life or sexual orientation . The additional requirements are given in Section 35 and 5 . Both require an appropriate policy document , and this document will form part of the policy for such processing , although competent authorities will need to satisfy themselves that their own internal policy documents fully cover such use .-->true_label--> <start> O O O O O O O O O B-ACTION O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O B-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE Det of data processing Data deposit by CO and data processing on London Data Platform occur as described in steps 1 & 2 of Schedule 2 . The parties responsible for analysis and sharing of data , as described in steps 3 & 4 of Schedule 2 , are the PR and London Borough of Walt Forest L . Data in the L Analysis F is accessed through a bearer token in Power B . Data is stored in cache in a Power B report , hosted by L . Only analysts employed by the PR or at L who need to develop the Power B report can access the data . The PR will share the Power B report with named individuals at 33 London Borough . Access to the report is individually password protected , with sharing to new individuals only possible by project staff at the PR . All analyses shared are at an aggregate level at which individual Borough can be identified . A S Me : This is the technical means by which parties shall transmit personal data between each other . It could be one or more of these listed below . EMI Health Systems Local Record S – Integrated Care : This shall be used to transmit patients medical records between partners to this agreement . The system will enable each EMI Partner share a patients medical record held on secure EMI Web clinical system in order to provide end of life p care services within the West Kent area . The information is accessed in real time and on - demand , meaning that data from the source GP record is neither extracted , nor uploaded , nor sent anywhere in real time and on - demand . Vision 360 : Vision 360 Practice Access provides secure , remote access to a patient ' s clinical data including medical history , therapy and test results . It allows Vision and EMI Web Practice you to share , view , record and edit patient consultation details between the two systems i of technological and organisation boundaries . MI : Healthcare Gateway is the system supplier of Medical Inter Gateway MI system that provides healthcare professionals with instant access to real - time information about a patient in order to make informed treatment decisions faster , and improve the efficiency of care by preventing unnecessary hospital admissions and du tests . 2 . 2 of information sharing through this agreement The purpose of this agreement is to allow information to be shared for the purpose of the prevention , reduction and detection crime and policing purposes within the area managed by The Partnership Organisation . College of policing Author Professional Practice AP on information management defines policing purposes as , protecting life and property , preserving order , preventing and detect offences , bringing offenders to justice , any duty or responsibility arising from common or statute law . For ease of drafting , F is used to cover both legislation . All requests for F will be directed through the relevant organisations F processes . Each party will seek advice from the other parties where there is concern about that information being released and any impact it is likely to have . Common Law Duty of Con Much of the police information to be shared will not have been obtained under a duty of confidence as it is legitimate assumed that data subjects will understand that police will act appropriately with regards to the information for the purposes of preventing harm to or promoting the welfare of children . However , for the police , as a safe before any information is passed on , it will undergo an assessment check against criteria included in Child Abu Investigation Command Standard Operating Pro by the Public Protection Des PP . Whilst still applying proportional and necessity to the decision , the protection of children or other vulnerable persons would clearly f a public interest test when passing the information to a partner agency whose work with the police would facilitate this aim . Information held by other agencies may have been gathered where a duty of confidence is owed . Duty of confidence is not an absolute bar to disclosure as information can be shared where there is a strong enough public interest to do so . When over the duty of confidential , the parties may seek the views of the organisation who hold the duty of confidential and consider their views in relation to breach confidential . Principles of information sharing Effect information sharing is a vital element of both early intervention and safe of vulnerable people at risk of harm or neglect . Organisation can hold different pieces of information which need to be placed together to enable a thorough assessment and plan to be made . To share information , a law basis for doing so must be identified . This may come from legislation or from statutory guidance such as Working Together to Safe Children or the Children Act 2004 , which places responsibilities on organisations outside of the parties to this DS , such as sports clubs , private organisations , and the voluntary , community and faith sectors . The sharing of personal data must comply with both the UK GDP Principles and the Cal Principles , listed at A A . Together , those principles lead to a series of questions and considerations to be answered before sharing takes place . OF There are practices in place between some organisations to share or maintain joint lists of vulnerable persons regularly for emergency prepared activity , i . e . rather than only gathering this information at the time of an emergency . Such information is also shared between some organisations to inform priority services for vulnerable customers , for example those offered by utility companies . These practices are related to sharing information about people affected by an emergency , however , they are not covered by this Data S Agreement . The categories of people affected by an emergency , or data subjects , include injured survivors , non - injured survivors , witnesses , be family and friends , and emergency respond see section 3 . 3 . for full list . Data to be shared may include personal information e . g . name and contact details , how the person is thought to have been affected i . e . their data subject category e . g . injured survivor , and special category data e . g . known vulnerability or medical need . Data would be shared between two or more organisations with a responsibility to offer or provide support services to the data subject . The organisations party to this Agreement are listed in A A , and the agreement is to be signed by all relevant parties , including local partners , voluntary sector , and any specialist organisations . By signing this Agreement , the named agencies agree to accept the conditions set out in this document , according to their statutory and professional responsibilities , and agree to ad to the procedures described . A E : Meeting Con State This statement or a locally agreed substitute will be read out at the beginning of each meeting . All attendees must comply , or identify to the Chair before the meeting begins if they are unable to comply , and why . The Chair will take the decision of whether the attendees can continue within the meeting . The Chair of the Meeting reminds all attendees of the requirements and protocols within the Integrated Off Management I Data S Agreement . The purpose of this meeting is for the relevant agencies to share information about offenders between themselves for the purpose of making , modify and implementing plans to reduce re and to protect the public from harm . The responsibility to take appropriate actions rests with the individual agencies . This includes responsibilities for the sharing of information under this agreement . All individuals who are discussed at the meeting will be treated fairly , with respect and without discrimination . All work at the meeting will be informed by a commitment to equal opportunities and effective practice issues in relation to race , gender , sexuality and disability , and by the duty to protect the confidential of the personal data of the individuals discussed . Information discussed within the meeting is confidential and must not be disclosed to third parties unless they are a sign to the I Data S Agreement , or when it has been agreed by the partners at the meeting . Hard copies of information brought to or received at the meeting will be left in the meeting room , where the I Co - or is responsible for secure disposal . All agencies must ensure that the minutes are retained secure in line with the agency retention schedule . Date of Meeting : By signing the attendance sheet agency representatives agree to a by this agreement . P and Ben This DS covers information sharing between councils , the Metropolitan Police , the City of London Police , and London Pro Services for Y - Youth Off Services . The scope described in sections 1 and 1 . 6 explain the information sharing that is covered by this agreement - that is information shared between councils Y , the Metropolitan Police , the City of London Police , and London Pro Services in respect of Youth Off Services . This includes prevent measures , interventions , refer , transfer to Adult Pro Service , and assessments . Research and experience have demonstrated the importance of information sharing across professional boundaries to ensure effective delivery of Y , better outcomes for young people and protection of the public . Lac of data sharing results in poor outcomes , including greater exposure of young people to danger and exploitation , increased crime , more victims and less public safety . Information viewed alone or in si may not give the full picture or identify the true risk . All the information from various agencies needs to be available and accessible to relevant agencies in one place ; to keep young people and members of the public safe and assist sign to this Agreement in disc their obligations under the relevant legislation that covers delivering Y . To deliver the best decisions that ensure time , necessary and proportion interventions , local authority decision makers need the full information picture concerning a young person , other relevant individuals and their circumstances to be available to them . The police who provide intelligence to the local authority on Y matters need appropriate information in order to make fully informed and evidenced representations , and to f their law and order responsibilities effectively . Police who may be called to deal with urgent issues regarding youth off may need time and up - to - date information to effectively handle such matters . Young offenders often find themselves in complex situations , causing them to be vulnerable to external influences . In order to provide a comprehensive service , Y professionals need to respond in real - time with up - to - date information . St and handling information secure Information should only be stored and shared in accordance with data protection legislation and follow information security policies and procedures of the relevant organisation . Information should always be shared secure , either by a secure IT connection , en email , or secure and tracked transfer of paper documents . Information should never be sent via a non - secure method . The employee sending the information must chose the most appropriate method of transfer and be responsible for its safe delivery . Em is not generally a secure method of transferring personal data . Although two or more of the parties may have additional encryption that allows for an en path between two of the parties , this cannot be identified simply from the email address . It would be p for parties to establish whether there are any en paths between them , and write that into the organisations processes for employees . In the absence of that , secure email systems such as C , E and En and Send must be used . Des of specific transfer processes must be in relevant process documents within each organisation . Information may be shared over the phone , in a virtual meeting , or a face to face meetings . Em must ensure that attendance and distribution of content is limited , with minutes or recordings with limited distribution . sharing by telephone should be avoided unless the requirement is urgent and email is not p . Any paper records printed must be kept to a minimum and kept secure at all times whether in the office , home or during transit . Organisation must adopt an appropriate policy surrounding the use and transfer of paper records . St and handling information secure Information must be stored and shared law and secure . Special category data may need a higher level of security . The employee sharing the information must choose the most appropriate secure method of transfer and be responsible for its safe delivery . Electronic records : Organisation may have different electronic methods for storing and sharing information secure . Some have local restrictions which block access to information shared using specific tools . Parties must make sure the chosen method is suit secure and that access is only provided to those who need it , and only to the data needed . Un email i . e . sent in plain text over the public internet must not be used to share information under this DS . Local Author up the child protection data onto the NHS Spin CP - IS system , which is operated by NHS Digital , to make this available to health bodies . This sharing between LA and health bodies via the Spin is for safe purposes . LA add the child protection data to the clinical record on Spin as Joint Controller for the child protection data . NHS Digital collects the audit data of the healthcare workers who have accessed the child protection data held in the clinical record on the NHS Spin CP - IS system . Common law duty of confidence Disc of information containing Personal Data will be conducted in compliance with the common law duty of confidence . quasi - experimental study . This will establish whether the intervention has the intended impact , and whether it represents value for money . To conduct a quasi - experimental study we have to collect personal data on those receiving the service and link it to data on outcomes . This cannot be achieved without processing personal sensitive data . A study of this nature also requires that special category data gender , sexuality , ethnicity , health be processed so that e can control for the impact of these on outcomes . Without this , we do not know whether the programme works as well for one group of people as for another . It is also necessary that criminal offence data be processed , as reducing the likelihood that a young person will go on to be convicted of an offence is a key outcome of the program and a driver of cost - efficacy . Without this , we cannot answer how effective the programme has been .-->true_label--> <start> O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE I-RULE_TYPE O B-ASSET I-ASSET O O O O O O O O O B-ASSET O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O B-ASSET O O O O O O O O O B-ASSET I-ASSET I-ASSET O O O O O O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O O O B-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O B-ASSET O O O O O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O B-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O O-->predict_label--> <start> O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE I-RULE_TYPE O B-ASSET I-ASSET O O O O O O O O O B-ASSET O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O B-ASSET O O O O O O O O O B-ASSET I-ASSET I-ASSET O O O O O O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O O O B-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O B-ASSET O O O O O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O B-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O O

Principles of information sharing Effect information sharing is a vital element of the Prevention & Det of Crime and Anti - Social Be . Organisation can hold different pieces of information which need to be placed together to enable a joined up approach problem solving . To share information , a law basis for doing so must be identified . This may come from legislation or from statutory guidance such as Crime & Di Act 1998 , amongst other relevant legislation , which established the formation of statutory Crime and Di Red Partnership CD in recognition of the idea that crime reduction cannot be the responsibility of one agency , such as the police and should be tackle by a variety of agencies working together in partnership . The sharing of personal data must comply with both the GDP Principles and the Cal Principles , listed at A B . Together , those principles lead to a series of questions and considerations to be answered before sharing takes place . These are listed as an Information S Check in A D : Information S Check .-->true_label--> <start> O O B-ASSET O O B-ASSET O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O B-ASSET O B-RULE_TYPE I-RULE_TYPE O O O B-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE O O B-ACTION B-ASSET O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O B-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT O O O O O O O O O O O B-ASSET I-ASSET B-RULE_TYPE B-ACTION O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O B-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT O O O O O O B-ASSET O O O O O O B-ASSET O O O-->predict_label--> <start> O O B-ASSET O O B-ASSET O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O B-ASSET O B-RULE_TYPE I-RULE_TYPE O O O B-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE O O B-ACTION B-ASSET O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O B-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT O O O O O O O O O O O B-ASSET I-ASSET B-RULE_TYPE B-ACTION O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O B-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT O O O O O O B-ASSET O O O O O O B-ASSET O O O
Freedom of Information F The Freedom of Information Act 2000 gives all individuals the right to access official information held by a public authority the Environmental Information Regulations 2004 also allow access to data . For ease of drafting , F is used to cover both legislation . Limited exemption may apply , and all public authorities must ensure they have recognised procedures in place for administer requests of this nature . All requests for F will be directed through the relevant organisations F processes . Each party will seek advice from the other parties where there is concern about that information being released and any impact it is likely to have . The final decision to disc or not will lie with the party who has the legal duty to respond to the request . It is encouraged that all parties pro publish this document . Right to be informed – P notices Where personal data is created or received by one of the parties , they are responsible , as required by law , for making the data subjects aware within a reasonable time frame that the organisation holds the data , what they will do with it , how long they will keep it , and who they will share it with such as under this DS . This is normally done through a privacy notice , whether written or verbal . Organisation agree that they will ad to the transparency requirements of the UK _ GDP and will issue appropriate notices which inform the data subject that the information will be shared with the parties under this agreement . In some cases , it may not be appropriate to let a person know that information about them is being processed and shared . Consider should be given to whether not the individual may place someone at risk or prejudice a police or safe investigation . In these circumstances , the parties need not inform individuals that the information is being processed ; but should record their reasons for sharing information without making the individual aware .-->true_label--> <start> O O O O O O B-ACTION O B-ASSET I-ASSET O O O O O O O O B-PARTY_ASSIGNEE O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O B-RULE_TYPE I-RULE_TYPE O O O O O B-ASSET O O B-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT O O O O O O O O B-ASSET O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE B-ACTION O O O O B-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT O O O O O O O O B-ACTION O O O O O O O B-ACTION O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O B-ACTION O B-ACTION O B-ASSET O O O B-ASSET B-RULE_TYPE O O O O B-PARTY_ASSIGNEE B-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE I-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O O B-ASSET O O O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O B-PARTY_ASSIGNEE B-RULE_TYPE O B-ACTION O O O B-ASSET O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE B-ASSET O O O O B-ASSET B-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT O-->predict_label--> <start> O O O O O O B-ACTION O B-ASSET I-ASSET O O O O O O O O B-PARTY_ASSIGNEE O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O B-RULE_TYPE I-RULE_TYPE O O O O O B-ASSET O O B-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT O O O O O O O O B-ASSET O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE B-ACTION O O O O B-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT O O O O O O O O B-ACTION O O O O O O O B-ACTION O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O B-ACTION O B-ACTION O B-ASSET O O O B-ASSET B-RULE_TYPE O O O O B-PARTY_ASSIGNEE B-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE I-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O O B-ASSET O O O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O B-PARTY_ASSIGNEE B-RULE_TYPE O B-ACTION O O O B-ASSET O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE B-ASSET O O O O B-ASSET B-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT O

Pro and necessity Pro and necessity are factors to be taken into consideration when deciding whether to share personal information . In making the decision , employees must weigh up what might happen as a result of the information being shared against what might happen if it is not , and apply their professional judgement . Although sharing of information can impact on a practitioners relationship with an individual , keeping the child safe must always be the first consideration . Safe is a special purpose under the Data Protection Act and as such you should share if the sharing is necessary for the protection of an individual , under or over 18 , who is at risk from neglect or physical , mental or emotional harm . P This agreement covers the sharing of information by and between the listed agencies for the purpose of preventing , de , investigating and prose environmental crimes . It is noted that there is an overlap between the responsibilities of the Environment Agency and local councils , and that information sharing between those is covered in a different DS and out the scope of this DS . The scope of environmental crime covers a wide range of activities from litter and dog foul , to fly tip which can include bulk disposal of contaminated materials , and illegal scrap metal activities . It also covers areas such as graffiti and similar def , abandoned vehicles and stray dogs . It is noted that bulk disposal of contaminated materials , and illegal scrap metal activities may be linked to Organ Criminal Gang activities . Whilst the other areas covered by this DS may not be considered to be serious offences , they are ones that can cause anxiety to residents and lead to de environmental effects for residents . Councils receive many complaints about graffiti and dog foul and these , and the other areas covered in this DS are ones that frequently negatively affect local residents . S information allows councils and agencies to take joined - up action and ensure the most appropriate course of action can be taken in respect of both individual cases and wider problems . Target actions can be coordinated , offenders located and prosecuted , and information shared to ensure that proportion outcomes are pursued .-->true_label--> <start> O O O O O O O B-ASSET Own of this agreement This agreement was drafted by a working group of representatives of the police , local authorities and London Councils . These professionals were specialists in the troubled families programme , police procedures , information governance and law . The local authority representatives worked under the banner of the Information Go for London Group I , to draft one agreement that would work for all boroughs and Metropolitan Police BC across London , and the City of London Police . The aim is to reduce the number of versions of sharing agreements that historically differed between boroughs , partly to reduce the burden on pan - London organisations that must have agreements with multiple boroughs . I , a group of information and security professionals at London boroughs , assisted with coordination of this agreement , but the responsibilities within it , and compliance with data protection legislation , remain with the listed Data Controller . Those purposes are at exercise of a function conferred on a person by an en or rule of law ; Pre or detect unlawful acts 101 This condition is met if the processing a necessary for the purposes of the prevention or detection of an unlawful act , b be carried out without the consent of the data subject so as not to prejudice those purposes , and c necessary for reasons of substantial public interest . For the purposes of law enforcement by competent authorities The competent authorities are defined in Section 30 of the D which refers to Schedule 7 . The competent authorities under this agreement are generally but not exclusively police , probation services , youth off teams and government departments . The law enforcement purposes are defined in Section 31 of the D as prevention , investigation , detection or prosecution of criminal offences or the execution of criminal penalties , including the safe against and the prevention of threats to public security . There are additional safe required for sensitive processing . This is defined in Section 35 as : a the processing of personal data revealing racial or ethnic origin , political opinions , religious or philosophical beliefs or trade union membership ; b the processing of genetic data , or of bio data , for the purpose of uniquely identifying an individual ; c the processing of data concerning health ; d the processing of data concerning an individuals sex life or sexual orientation . The additional requirements are given in Section 35 and 5 . Both require an appropriate policy document , and this document will form part of the policy for such processing , although competent authorities will need to satisfy themselves their own internal policy documents fully cover such use . Section 35 requires the consent of the data subject , 35 requires that the processing be strictly necessary for the law enforcement purposes and meets a condition in Schedule 8 . Data breach All parties must have a clear policy and procedure regarding the reporting and handling of data protection breach or data loss incidents . This must include assessing the level of risk to the data subjects , as well as to make a decision on not the I within the statutory time frame of 72 hours . This com with Articles 33 and 34 of UK , and Section 67 and 68 of the D 2018 for personal data processed for law enforcement purposes . The decision to report the incident will lie with the data controllers of the information concerned . The parties agree to provide all reasonable and necessary assistance at their own expense to each other to facilitate the handling of any personal data breach in an ex and com manner . Freedom of Information The Freedom of Information Act 2000 gives all individuals the right to access official information held by a public authority the Environmental Information Regulations 2004 also allow access to data . For ease of drafting , F is used to cover both legislation . Limited exemption may apply and all public authorities must ensure they have recognised procedures in place for administer requests of this nature . All requests for F will be directed through the relevant organisations F processes . Each party will seek advice from the other parties where there is concern about that information being released and any impact it is likely to have . The final decision to disc or not will lie with the party who received the request as the legal duty lies with them . In order to ensure that the authority in re of the F request is able to respond within the statutory deadline , any request for assistance or information made to partner authorities should be made and processed within two working days , and any data exchange completed within seven working days . It is encouraged that all parties pro publish this document . It may also be disclosed to the public under F .-->true_label--> <start> O O B-ASSET O O O B-ASSET O O B-ACTION O O O O O O O B-ASSET O B-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT O O O O B-ASSET O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O [ Yes ] Nature and purpose of the information sharing 4 . 1 Parties consider this information sharing initiative necessary as it is vital to ensure that Croydon Council can adequately audit providers to ensure that H clubs have been accessed by children who require Free School Me , and that returns to the Department for Education are accurate . Virtual Private Networks The above are examples , get advice from your organisations information security or IT teams on secure methods of sharing available at your organisation and document these in the organisations process documents . Phone meetings - to - face meetings : Information may be shared over the phone , in a virtual meeting , or at face to face meetings . Meeting attendance and distribution of content , e . g . meeting minutes or recordings , must be limited to those with a need to know . S by telephone should be avoided unless the requirement is urgent and email is not p . Individuals should be aware of their surroundings and the presence of other individuals or voice recognition or Internet of Things devices e . g . virtual assistant apps like Alex , Co , S to ensure they aren overheard by those that should not have access to the information discussed . Paper records : Print paper records must always be kept to a minimum and kept secure whether in the office , home or during transit . MA London Project Information sharing agreement It can reasonably be assumed that the persons from whom information is obtained will legitimate expect that police will share it appropriately with any person or agency that will assist in fulfilling the policing purposes mentioned above . If possible , and as far as is consistent with the welfare of the child concerned , explicit consent will be obtained by Family Services and Social Work before individual cases are brought to the MA . In these cases , where consent has been granted , individuals will have a legitimate expectation of how their data is going to be used and with whom it may be shared and why . Human Rights - Article 8 : The right to respect for private and family life , home and correspondence There shall be no interference by a public authority with the exercise of this right except such as is in accordance with the law and is necessary in a democratic society in the interests of national security , public safety or the economic well - being of the country , for the prevention of disorder or crime , for the protection of health or moral , or for the protection of the rights and freedom of others . Con is relevant to the rights of those to whom confidential information relates , and thus to legal obligations such as the Human Rights Act 1998 . The sharing of the information with Children Safe and Social Work may engage Article 8 , however there will be no con provided that an exception within Article 82 applies . The benefits of effective information sharing for the purposes set out in this Agreement are to the direct benefit of the citizen and so in the public interest . This Agreement is : In pursuit of a legitimate aim – The promotion of the safety and welfare of children and ensuring they achieve all five outcomes is , by virtue of S . 11 of Children Act 2004 , a legitimate aim and major responsibility of the Partners to this Agreement . The sharing of information under this Agreement is also in line with Articles 2 and 3 of the Human Rights Act 1988 , namely the right to life and the right to prohibition of torture or in or de treatment . Data breach All parties must have a clear policy and procedure regarding the reporting and handling of data protection breach or data loss incidents . This must include assessing the level of risk to the data subjects , as well as to make a decision on not the I within the statutory time frame of 72 hours . Common Law Duty of Con Much of the police information to be shared will not have been obtained under a duty of confidence as it is legitimate assumed that data subjects will understand that police will act appropriately with regards to the information for the purposes of preventing harm to or promoting the welfare of children . However , for the police , as a safe before any information is passed on , it will undergo an assessment check against criteria included in relevant Standard Operating Pro by the Public Protection Des PP . Whilst still applying proportional and necessity to the decision , the protection of persons at risk would clearly f a public interest test when passing the information to a partner agency whose work with the police would facilitate this aim . Information held by other agencies that will be shared in the Pre process may have been gathered where a duty of confidence is owed . Duty of confidence is not an absolute bar to disclosure as information can be shared where consent has been provided or where there is a strong enough public interest to do so . When over the duty of confidential , the parties may seek the views of the organisation who hold the duty of confidential and consider their views in relation to breach confidential . Virtual Private Networks The above are examples , get advice from your organisations information security or IT teams on secure methods of sharing available at your organisation and document these in the organisations process documents . Phone meetings - to - face meetings : Information may be shared over the phone , in a virtual meeting , or at face to face meetings . Meeting attendance and distribution of content , e . g . meeting minutes or recordings , must be limited to those with a need to know . S by telephone should be avoided unless the requirement is urgent and email is not p . Individuals should be aware of their surroundings and the presence of other individuals or voice recognition or Internet of Things devices e . g . virtual assistant apps like Alex , Co , S to ensure they aren overheard by those that should not have access to the information discussed . Paper records : Print paper records must always be kept to a minimum and kept secure whether in the office , home or during transit . Conference Protocol Fr Each D MA will decide on the necessary frequency of conferences , to discuss high - risk cases and share information about the parties involved to allow action to be taken to manage the risk posed to the victim and any children . This includes emergency MA . Information sharing The information will be shared between designated and named representatives from the sign parties , and with other agencies identified as relevant by the local D MA Co - or on a case - by - case basis . These agencies include community based and voluntary per programmes , local drug and alcohol services , child and family support organisations . Con Con of the MA is para . At the start of each MA the Chair will read out the confidential agreement , which all attendees are expected to follow ; that information discussed within the meeting is sensitive and must not be disclosed to a third party without the agreement of the parties at the meeting . Any virtual attendees have responsibility for confidential of the meeting , such as ensuring they are in a location where they cannot be overheard , and their screen cannot be seen . The MA Chair should remind attendees of this responsibility before the meeting begins . De . of Justice , II The I In Programme II enables non - E nationals to secure immigration permission to Ireland on the basis of long - term investment of a sum of money in a project that has been approved for that purpose by the Department of Justice and other Government stakeholders . The function of the Exchange of Information E Branch , Office of the Revenue Commissioners , is to exchange relevant information with tax administration in other jurisdictions in accordance with Revenue commitments under international agreements such as the CR . Office of the The CR is the global standard for the Automatic Exchange Of Revenue Information A regarding financial accounts between tax Commissioners authorities . The E Branch spontaneous exchanges relevant information relating to successful II applicants with their jurisdiction of tax residence under relevant legal instruments . This allows Ireland to meet its commitments under the CR , maintaining its integrity and effectiveness . Introduction to the Data S Agreement The London Re Forum L sets the strategy for the work of the London Re Partnership . Local Re Forum were introduced in 2004 in the Civil Con Act to provide the means for those involved in emergency prepared to collaborate at a local level . The L ensures London prepared in the event of emerge and coordinates the activities of a wide range of organisations to achieve this . It also provides a link between emergency prepared and re at the local and national levels . The London Re Partnership comprises more than 200 organisations . These include local authorities , emergency services police , health organisations , utility companies , transport providers , and representatives of the business , faith , and voluntary sectors . As well as the L , a Borough Re Forum BR is maintained in each London Local Authority area . The Borough Re Forum enable cooperation and information sharing between re partners to support emergency prepared at the Borough level . The L and BR maintain a range of emergency response capabilities that will be supported by this Data S Agreement DS , such as those set out in the London Human Assistance Framework , and Borough - level arrangements to provide humanitarian assistance to people affected by an emergency . These arrangements would be coordinated by a Human Assistance St Group H established for the emergency which would agree information sharing requirements between H members as required to support the humanitarian response . This DS documents how the parties to this agreement will share personal data about people affected by an emergency with organisations that have a responsibility to undertake safe actions and offer or provide support services to those people , such as humanitarian assistance . Introduction This guidance note will take you through steps to consider your data sharing needs , whether a data sharing agreement is right for you and what to include . This guide was originally developed in partnership with CA and GO . It draws on the experiences of those organisations in participating in data sharing agreements for a programmes . While the guide has been written to be more generally applicable , we welcome feedback on the content from those working in other contexts to help us improve it . Wanted Off Whilst MA is a victim focused process , the sharing of information around wanted individuals per domestic abuse is an essential part of victim safe . By sharing information held around wanted D offenders with the police and other statutory bodies , victim risk can be successfully reduced . The police can use the MA framework to request information from partners on a wanted off and their whereabouts to effect law arrests . Success app and detention will reduce risk for victims and will reduce the volume of D MA cases . This information sharing can take place in writing , at a MA panel or across a virtual space if secure . Re will be made as necessary . Assessment and review A review of this information sharing agreement will take place yearly , unless otherwise agreed by the organisations Data Protection Officers . The aim of the review will be to ensure the purposes are still relevant , the scope has not slipped , the benefits to the data subjects and organisations are being realised , and the procedures followed for information security are effective . Changes in legislation and developments in the areas of public sector data sharing will be considered as and when they arise , as will any changes to the sign parties .-->true_label--> <start> O O O O O O O B-ASSET O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O B-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT O B-ASSET O O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O B-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE O O O O O O O O O O O B-ASSET O O O O O O O O O O O O B-ASSET O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O B-ASSET O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O B-PARTY_ASSIGNEE O-->predict_label--> <start> O O O O O O O B-ASSET O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O B-CONSTRAINT I-CONSTRAINT O B-ASSET O O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O B-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE I-PURPOSE O O O O O O O O O O O B-ASSET O O O O O O O O O O O O B-ASSET O O O O O O O O O O O O O O O B-ASSET O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O O O O O B-RULE_TYPE O O O O O B-PARTY_ASSIGNEE O

Duty of confidence A duty of confidence may be owed to both the holder of the data and to the data subject . Much of the police information to be shared will not have been obtained under a duty of confidence as it is legitimate assumed that data subjects will understand that the police will act appropriately with regards to the information for the purposes of preventing harm to or promoting the welfare of children . However , as a safe before any information is passed on , it will undergo an assessment check against criteria included in Child Abu Investigation Command Standard Operating Pro by the MA Public Protection Des MA PP . Whilst still applying proportional and necessity to the decision , the protection of children or other vulnerable persons would clearly f a public interest test when passing the information to a Partner whose work with the police would facilitate this aim . All information shared with a Partner must be relevant to the case Information held by the London Borough of Camden and other organisations that will be shared in the MA may have been gathered where a duty of confidence is owed . Duty of confidence is not an absolute bar to disclosure , as information can be shared where consent has been provided or where there is a strong enough public interest to do so , for example where there is an over need to protect a child welfare , or a serious crime has been committed or by order of the Court MA IS : Is January 2017 Page 7 of 37 Review Date : 31 December 2017 © Social Worker Work Analysis Nature and purpose of analysis This schedule provides details of the data flow for Social Worker Work Census data through the L platform . The data submitted by the CO is the Children Social Work Work Census . The process will enable pan - London analysis to understand how the pan - London labour market for registered social workers is functioning for staff and employers . The purpose of the analysis is to collaborate with other authorities to improve the labour market for registered social workers e . g . by reducing reliance on agency staff , reducing staff turnover and ensuring equal opportunity for BA staff . Manual data flow Audi data and Power B Dash NHS Digital operates as a platform to allow LA and Health to share data . One of the purposes of the CP - IS system is also to provide reporting and monitoring data about the Child Protection Information S CP - IS system nationally . This is required both to provide the CP - IS Board with data to manage delivery and to provide Healthcare and Local Author with a single national and regional view of safe data and visibility of what data is held on the NHS Spin . Assessment and review A review of this information sharing agreement will take place every two years , unless otherwise agreed by the organisations Data Protection Officers . The aim of the review will be to ensure the purposes are still relevant , the scope has not slipped , the benefits to the data subjects and organisations are being realised , and the procedures followed for information security are effective . Changes in legislation and developments in the areas of public sector data sharing will be considered as and when they arise , as will any changes to the sign parties . MA London Project Information sharing agreement We the under agree that each Partner that we represent will adopt and ad to this Agreement : The Partners agree that if a new organisation wishes to become a Partner to this Agreement , the new organisation will be required to obtain the written consent of all the Partners to this Agreement . Law Ba for Process Special Category Personal Data In order to law share special category personal data , both an Article 6 law basis as set out above , and one additional condition for processing data from Article 92 GDP must be met . Fair and Trans A basic requirement of Data Protection Law is that individuals should be told who is holding their information , what the Controller intends to do with the information and who else they may share it with . For the purposes of this agreement both Part are Controller of the Personal Data they hold , including Data that they receive from each other . Each Part must ensure that they have appropriate policies and procedures in place to facilitate both the protection and the ex of rights of individuals under UK data protection legislation . Official subject of the data the data subject has the right to know who holds their data and how such data are or will be processed , including how such data are to be shared . Sen data Certain types of data are referred to as sensitive personal data . These are data which relate to the data subjects : Ra or ethnic origin ; Political opinions ; Religious beliefs , or other beliefs of a similar nature ; Trade union membership ; Physical or mental health or condition ; Sexual life ; Commission or alleged commission of any offence ; Any proceedings for any offence committed , or alleged to have been committed . Additional and more string obligations and restrictions apply whenever sensitive personal data is processed . Personal confidential data In 2013 the Department of Health published the Cal Information Go Review , which was an independent review of how information about patients is shared across the health and care system . The review introduced the term personal confidential data to describe personal and sensitive information about identified or id individuals , which should be kept private or secret , and includes deceased as well as living people . This afford protection under information governance processes to personally id information relating to deceased persons , as such data is outside the scope of the Data Protection Act . The Cal Information Go Review can be found at : https : www . go . uk _ data _ I n _ a . p The term personal confidential data describes personal and sensitive information relating to identified or id individuals , whether living or deceased . For the purposes of this Agreement , personal includes the Data Protection Act definition of personal data , but it is adapted to include deceased as well as living people and confidential includes both information given in confidence and that which is owed a duty of confidence and is adapted to include sensitive data as defined in the Data Protection Act . Assessment and review A review of this information sharing agreement will take place every two years , unless otherwise agreed by the organisations Data Protection Officers . The aim of the review will be to ensure the purposes are still relevant , the scope has not slipped , the benefits to the data subjects and organisations are being realised , and the procedures followed for information security are effective . Data hosting , flows and security Con The London Data Platform is a proposed data - sharing platform created by a partnership between L , the Greater London Authority , and Social Finance , a not - for - profit consult with substantial technical and information governance expertise . It is required because local public services , most notably local authority children services , are increasingly finding it necessary to share personal sensitive data with one another and with suppliers in order to deliver on their statutory responsibilities . Host data within the London Data Platform and running analysis within its secure environment will allow for increased data security , as it drastically reduces the risk of data breach associated with email transfers , data downloads or holding data locally . The architecture of the platform has been designed specifically with this use - case in mind and it holds data security as its core principle . This architecture is detailed in Data security below . Social Finance will act as architectural principal for this project and will thus be responsible for the role of primary security contact and s . Amazon Web Services will be the service provider . Det of the appropriate certification are outlined in the sections below . authorities to promote economic , social and environmental well - being within their boundaries . This was mostly replaced by the Local Act 2011 below , but still applies in Wales . The Local Act 2011 The Local Act created general powers for Local Government to act as an individual for any purpose , with specific goals to promote economic , social and environmental well - being within their boundaries . This regulation provides the general power for local authorities to act in any manner they believe suitable for the purposes giving a legal basis under Section 8 of the D for this use . However , as a general power it can be challenged , and an additional legal basis is preferred . The Education Act 2002 The Education Act 2002 puts a duty on schools to exercise their functions with a view to safe and promoting the welfare of children . All schools are required by law to teach a broad and balanced curriculum which promotes the spiritual , moral and cultural development of pupils and prepares them for the opportunities , responsibilities and experiences of life . This regulation provides specific powers for dealing with school - related safe and welfare issues giving a legal basis under Section 8 of the D for this use . The Children Act 1989 Under S . 47 of the Act 1989 , a Local Authority has a duty to investigate when informed that a child in their area is in police protection or the subject of a protection order . This regulation provides specific powers giving a legal basis under Section 8 of the D for this use . Other Non - Core Age may be identified and approved by the MA Chair . These organisations may be contracted by the core partners to deliver services such as victim support and refuge . These organisations are usually considered data controllers in their own right when delivering this contracted work , due to their independence and professional responsibilities . Guest Age Other agencies may be invited to attend or supply information to the MA to provide relevant information on a case and assist in the development and execution of the risk management plan . These will be guest agencies to the MA and can attend if invited . Guest agencies only attend for the case discussion of the specific cases they are attending for and only receive the relevant minutes and actions . Data hosting , flows and security Con The London Data Platform is a proposed data - sharing platform created by a partnership between L , the Greater London Authority , and Social Finance , a not - for - profit consult with substantial technical and information governance expertise . It is required because local public services , most notably local authority children services , are increasingly finding it necessary to share personal sensitive data with one another and with suppliers in order to deliver on their statutory responsibilities . Host data within the London Data Platform and running analysis within its secure environment will allow for increased data security , as it drastically reduces the risk of data breach associated with email transfers , data downloads or holding data locally . The architecture of the platform has been designed specifically with this use - case in mind and it holds data security as its core principle . This architecture is detailed in Data security below . Social Finance will act as architectural principal for this project and will thus be responsible for the role of primary security contact and s . Amazon Web Services will be the service provider . Det of the appropriate certification are outlined in the sections below . Common law duty of confidential The common law duty of confidential is engaged when there is a disclosure of information that risks breach the confidential of the individual to whom the information relates , where they would not expect those seeing the data to have access , regardless of whether there is any harm that comes of that or not . In terms of processing for analytical activities there are a number of factors that will ensure that the risk of a confidential breach is sufficiently mini . These are : Data from the shared care record used for direct care will be copied , combined with other data sources and segregated into the three data as illustrated in section 4 . 2 and access to these data will be robust controlled : I data : for the use of intelligence data in relation to individuals i . e . , risk s case finding to support direct care provision . This is in essence provision of direct care and so the application of common law of confidential is the same as the section on direct care . Po non direct care uses of this data are referenced in section 4 . 2 and subject to specific D that will consider confidential on a case - by - case basis . P data : Use of this data will not be able to identify the individual and there is no disclosure of confidential data . Articles 6 1 , 9 and 10 of the UK GDP , and section 8 of the D 2018 set out the acceptable conditions for the processing and sharing of personal , special category , and criminal data . The conditions relevant in the UK GDP to data processed under this agreement are below . Article 6 1 – Personal Data Process c processing is necessary for compliance with a legal obligation to which the controller is subject . This applies to non - local authority sign to the DS . Section 38 of the CT & S Act amended by the Counter - Terrorism and Border Security Act 2019 , requires Channel partners to co - operate with the local authority and the police in providing any relevant information to the panel so that they can effectively carry out their functions to determine whether an individual is vulnerable to being drawn into terrorism e Process for the purposes of Channel relies on the law basis for processing set out in Article 61 GDP : the processing is necessary for the performance of a task carried out in the public interest or in the exercise of official authority vest in the controller . Also includes a processing of personal data that is necessary for the exercise of a function conferred on a person by an en or rule of law , and b processing of personal data that is necessary for the exercise of a function of the Crown , a Minister of the Crown or a government department . IF YOU WA TO K M CO E Data is collected on behalf of the Ministry of Housing , Communities and Local Go er M for research and statistical purposes only . The processing must have a law basis which , in this case , is that the processing is necessary for the performance of a task carried out in the public interest to meet a function of the Crown , a Minister of the Crown or a government department . Data providers do not require the consent of tenants to provide the information but tenants have the right to know how and for what purpose your data is being collected , held and used . You have the right to object and you have the right to obtain confirmation that your data is being processed , and to access your personal data . You also have the right to have any incorrect personal data corrected . The information collected via CO relates to your ten , the dwelling you are living in or buying , and your household . Some of the information may have been provided by you as a tenant when signing the new ten or buying your property ; other has been gathered from the housing management systems of social housing providers . Data collected will be held for as long as necessary for research and statistical purposes . When no longer needed , CO data will be destroyed in a safe manner . We are aware that some of the data collected is particularly sensitive : ethnic group ; previous tenure in hospital or prison probation host support ; if household left last settled home because discharged from prison long stay hospital other institution ; or if source of refer is probation prison , youth off team , community mental health team or health service . Please rest assured that all the information collected via CO is treated in accordance with Data Protection requirements and guidelines , and used anonymous . Data is published by M in aggregate form on an annual basis as part of a report and complementary tables . To access the annual publications on letting please visit https : www . go . uk - letting - and - ten ; To see the publications on sales please visit https : www . go . uk - sales --->true_label-->
